Megaplan bakeoff already has the healthier pattern: isolated worktrees plus explicit merge selection.\n \n Why this matters:\n For this style of chained work, each sprint needs an isolation boundary. Otherwise \"Sprint 2 quality\" and \"Sprint 1 quality\" become entangled.\n@@\n - each sprint runs in its own branch/worktree;\n - successful sprint output is reviewed and merged into a known integration branch;\n - the next sprint starts from that integration branch;\n - generated Megaplan state stays outside the app diff or is ignored.\n+\n+Implementation hint:\n+Reuse the bakeoff worktree/merge machinery instead of inventing a second isolation model.\n \n ### 9. Chain state/status can be misleading after retries\n \n Severity: medium\n \n Examples observed:\n - `chain_state.json` initially retained `last_state: \"stalled\"` after a manual restart while the plan was actually healthy.\n - Sprint 2 status showed all tasks complete while active step remained `execute`.\n - `last_step` can show an earlier timeout/error while a repair execute is currently healthy.\n+\n+Additional evidence:\n+Cloud chain uploads the spec to a stable remote path (`/workspace/reigh-app/chain.yaml`), and core chain state is stored beside it as `chain_state.json`. Re-running a different local chain in the same workspace can therefore reuse stale state unless the operator manually resets it.\n \n Why this matters:\n Humans need to know whether a plan is done, repairing, stalled, or safe to review. Current status requires interpretation across `state`, `active_step`, `last_step`, `progress`, and `chain_state`.\n@@\n - `repairing`\n - `waiting_review`\n - `done`\n - `failed`\n - `stalled`\n \n When a repair starts, show: \"repairing after review failure\" or \"retrying after execute timeout\" instead of leaving that inference to the operator.\n+\n+Also add:\n+- namespaced remote specs/state by chain id or run id;\n+- a spec hash in `chain_state.json`;\n+- `cloud chain --reset-state`;\n+- `cloud chain --resume-state`.\n@@\n ### 11. Railway API/SSH timeouts interrupt observability\n \n Severity: medium\n \n Examples observed:\n - Railway GraphQL request timeout during status polling.\n - Railway WebSocket timeout while reading artifacts.\n+\n+Additional evidence:\n+Provider commands in the Railway/SSH layers use subprocess calls without a consistent timeout envelope. A provider timeout is therefore easy to confuse with plan failure or a hung command.\n \n Why this matters:\n The plan can be healthy while the operator sees provider errors. That invites unnecessary restarts.\n \n Fix direction:\n Add retry/backoff to `megaplan cloud exec/status`, and prefer reading cached status artifacts from the worker when possible. Provider failures should be reported as \"observer failed, plan state unknown\" rather than plan failure.\n+\n+Add provider-level configurable timeouts and classify:\n+- timeout;\n+- nonzero exit;\n+- missing CLI;\n+- transient provider/API failure.\n@@\n ### 13. Timeout/retry semantics need clearer finalization\n \n Severity: medium\n \n Problem:\n Sprint 2 had an execute timeout, later review returned `needs_rework`, then a repair execute started. Status exposed these facts, but the lifecycle was not obvious.\n+\n+Additional evidence:\n+Auto phase timeout is recorded as an exit code and later interpreted through stall/retry behavior. Chain-level status receives a terminal driver outcome but loses structured timeout class/detail.\n \n Why this matters:\n An operator needs to know whether the plan is still attempting repair, whether the review failed, and whether it will automatically review again after repair.\n@@\n - `attempt: 2`\n - `reason: review_failed`\n - `blocking_findings: [...]`\n - next expected phase after execute: `review`\n+\n+Timeouts should be first-class outcomes with phase, command, elapsed time, retry policy, and whether the worker process was killed.\n+\n+### 14. Existing cloud chain sessions are ambiguous\n+\n+Severity: low/medium\n+\n+Problem:\n+When `cloud chain` finds an existing remote tmux session, it can report that the chain is already running without proving which spec, state file, or chain id the tmux session is using.\n+\n+Why this matters:\n+This is risky after uploading a new chain spec. The operator may believe the new chain is running while the old tmux session is still executing an older spec.\n+\n+Fix direction:\n+Persist active chain metadata in the remote workspace and show it in `cloud status --chain`:\n+- chain id/spec path;\n+- spec hash;\n+- started at;\n+- current plan;\n+- tmux session id.\n+\n+Require an explicit `--replace` flag to kill/restart an existing chain session.\n*** End Patch\n"
```json
}
```

> AGENT

> AGENT

It found/fixed issues through the normal Megaplan review → execute repair loop.

What happened:

1. **Sprint 2 first executed successfully**
   It completed `7/7` tasks and produced `execution.json`.

2. **Review found real failures**
   `review.json` returned `needs_rework`, specifically:
   - frontend command facade duplicated `commands/core.ts`;
   - `registerAsset` behavior had drifted;
   - broad tests were not green.

3. **Megaplan re-entered execute**
   After `needs_rework`, it ran `megaplan execute` again against the same plan. That repair pass targeted the review’s rework items:
   - T4: command facade/shared core issue;
   - T6: broad verification failures;
   - T7: manual smoke checklist remains human-only.

4. **Repair execution says the issues are now fixed**
   The later `execution.json` says:
   - `commands/index.ts` now imports command implementations from `./core`;
   - mounted facade only adapts shared results into `commitData` / selection behavior;
   - it added regression coverage for `registerAsset` when `registry.assets` starts undefined;
   - it reran the previously red tests plus `npm run test:edge` and full `npm test`, and says they passed.

I also checked the actual current code: `src/tools/video-editor/commands/index.ts` now imports `addClip`, `registerAsset`, etc. from `./core` and wraps them in `createTimelineCommandFacade()`. So that specific architectural issue does look repaired.

What it’s doing now: **nothing. It is stopped.**

Current state:
- Plan: `video-editor-developer-20260502-1920`
- State: `executed`
- Next step: `review`
- Active process: none
- Chain state: `stalled`

Why stuck:
- After the repair execute, Megaplan tried `review` five times.
- Each review attempt produced output, but the harness rejected it with:
  `Output file ... was not valid JSON and no fallback found`
- The raw review content appears to say “Approved. All must criteria pass,” but because it wasn’t parseable JSON, Megaplan could not transition the plan to `done`.
- After five failed review attempts, chain stall detection stopped Sprint 2 and did not advance to Sprint 3.

So semantically: **Sprint 2 may be fixed.**
Mechanically: **Megaplan is stuck on review-output parsing, not currently working.**

> AGENT

The second run did see the first run’s issue and repaired it.

What happened:

1. Sprint 2 first review correctly returned `needs_rework`.
   It found a real issue: `commands/index.ts` had drifted from the pure command core, especially around `registerAsset`.

2. Megaplan ran a repair execute pass.
   The later cloud worktree now shows `commands/index.ts` imports the shared implementations from `./core`, and the repair added regression coverage. So the app-code issue was actually fixed.

3. The next review approved the work, but Megaplan rejected the approval.
   I checked the actual temp output files in `/tmp`. They are syntactically valid JSON and say:

   ```json
   "review_verdict": "approved"
   ```

   But they are missing one required schema key:

   ```text
   missing ['checks']
   ```

4. Megaplan reports that as:

   ```text
   Output file tmp... was not valid JSON and no fallback found
   ```

   That message is misleading. The JSON was valid. It failed Megaplan’s review payload validation because the prompt/schema are out of sync.

The root issue is **Megaplan review schema drift**:

- `schemas.py` requires review outputs to include `checks`.
- The active review prompt’s example shape does not include `checks`.
- Codex followed the prompt and returned a sensible approved review without `checks`.
- Megaplan rejected it before writing `review.json`.
- Since `review.json` stayed as the old `needs_rework` file, the chain never advanced and eventually stalled in `state=executed`, `next_step=review`.

So the blocker is not that the second run ignored the first review. It repaired the first review. The blocker is that Megaplan could not accept the successful final review because its own review contract is internally inconsistent.

The fix should be in Megaplan:

- Align `review.json` schema and review prompt.
- Either always require and show `checks` in the prompt, or make `checks` optional/backfilled for the newer criteria/task/sense-check review shape.
- Change the parser error to preserve the real reason, e.g. `review output missing required keys: checks`, instead of saying “not valid JSON.”
- Add a regression test for: valid approved review JSON missing `checks` should either normalize cleanly or fail with a precise validation error.

That is the bottom of it. The cloud worker did run the repair; the chain got stuck because Megaplan rejected a correct-enough approval payload due to its own schema/prompt mismatch.

> DEVELOPER

Is all this code in reigh-app?

> AGENT

No.

There are two separate codebases involved:

- `reigh-app`: the target app being modified by the cloud chain.
  Current cloud path: `/workspace/reigh-app`
  Local path: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app`

- `megaplan`: the orchestration tool that runs the chain.
  Local fix path: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan`

> AGENT

Short answer: **not reliably**.

The local `reigh-app` checkout looks mostly aligned with the shared `@banodoco/timeline-schema` / ArtAgents contract, but the **cloud worker’s in-progress `/workspace/reigh-app` tree does not**. The cloud tree still has the older closed video-editor model:

- `ClipType = 'media' | 'hold' | 'text' | 'effect-layer'`
- no shared `@banodoco/timeline-schema` re-export in `src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts`
- serializer only allows top-level `output`, `clips`, `tracks`, `pinnedShotGroups`
- no `theme`, `theme_overrides`, `generation_defaults`
- no clip-level `params`, `pool_id`, `clip_order`, `source_uuid`, `generation`

That means the current cloud sprint work can drift away from ArtAgents / shared timeline schema, especially for custom clip types and Banodoco provenance fields.

The subagent also found real boundary issues:

- There is no local `reigh-timeline` repo checkout; the real shared contract appears to be `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-schema`.
- DB storage itself is permissive: `public.timelines.config jsonb`, `asset_registry jsonb`.
- But `timeline-import` has problems:
  - create path likely fails because DB requires `user_id` and `name`, but insert omits them.
  - it validates with Zod but stores raw payload, so unknown keys may pass import then later fail Reigh load validation.
- ArtAgents supports URL-only assets, but Reigh expects `asset_registry.assets[*].file`; URL-only assets can fail preview/load in Reigh.
- ArtAgents requires `theme` for render validation, while Reigh allows no-theme timelines.
- Shared schema allows richer transition references than Reigh’s renderer currently understands.

So the answer is: **the intended local contract is aligned, but the cloud execution has regressed or is based on an older Reigh timeline model.** I would treat this as a blocker for the current Megaplan chain before letting Sprint 3+ continue too far. The next fix should be to make the cloud `reigh-app` tree restore the shared schema boundary and add explicit cross-repo contract tests for Reigh ⇄ ArtAgents import/load/round-trip.

> AGENT

Yes: the root cause is **the cloud worker did not successfully move onto the latest `reigh-app` code before executing the chain**.

Evidence:

- Local/current `reigh-app` is on:
  - branch `sprint-8-themed-render-via-orchestrator`
  - commit `c99e760e`
  - includes the newer schema-aligned video-editor types.

- Cloud worker is on:
  - branch `megaplan/m1b-m1c-stores`
  - commit `d3e000d`
  - an older branch where video-editor still has the closed `ClipType` union and older serializer allowlists.

- The chain log already showed the refresh failed:
  - `git checkout main -> rc=1`
  - `git pull --ff-only origin main -> rc=1`

So the chain continued anyway from the stale branch.

I would not describe this as “the plan introduced the regression” in the usual sense. It did not take latest aligned code and break it. It started from an old baseline that never had the schema-lift alignment. But if we merge the cloud work as-is, it would effectively reintroduce old assumptions and become a regression relative to current `reigh-app`.

The fix path should be:

1. Stop/pause the current chain before Sprint 3 goes deeper.
2. Preserve the cloud work as a patch/branch.
3. Start from latest `origin/main` or the intended current branch/commit.
4. Re-apply/cherry-pick only the useful Sprint 1/2 changes onto that base.
5. Add a guard so `megaplan chain` aborts if `git checkout` / `git pull` fails, instead of continuing on a stale dirty branch.

> DEVELOPER

How confident are you that we nailed this? Do we need to think through how megaplan managed git state?

> AGENT

I’m confident we found and fixed the immediate cause: the cloud worker was on a stale branch, the chain’s git refresh failed, and Megaplan continued anyway. The fresh run is now on `origin/main` with the expected schema-lifted video-editor types, and the stale work is backed up.

But I’m **not** confident Megaplan’s git-state model is “nailed” yet. We fixed the dangerous failure mode, not the design.

What still needs thinking through:

- Megaplan currently mutates one shared checkout across milestones.
- It can leave tracked changes in the app repo, like `.megaplan/schemas/review.json`.
- Chain specs are untracked runtime files inside the target repo.
- It refreshes `main` between milestones, which conflicts with dirty work unless that work is committed or isolated.
- Backup/merge semantics are manual.
- The chain does not yet have a clean “worktree per milestone, merge on approval” model.

The better abstraction is probably:

- Treat `origin/main` as immutable input per milestone.
- Run each milestone in its own branch/worktree.
- Persist chain metadata outside the app repo or under ignored `.megaplan/runtime`.
- Fail before execution if base checkout is dirty in tracked files.
- Have first-class merge outcomes:
  `completed_unmerged`, `merged`, `needs_manual_merge`, `discarded`.
- Require explicit promotion from a milestone branch back to the integration branch.

So: yes, we fixed the live incident. But yes, we should think through Megaplan git state. The current model is too easy to accidentally run good automation from the wrong tree.

> AGENT

Right now: the cloud task is progressing. Sprint 1 is on the fresh `origin/main` baseline, plan + critique + gate have completed, and the gate routed it into `revise`. The active step is healthy and only just started. Chain status is still:

`Sprint 1 canonical data model -> in progress`
`Sprints 2-8 -> pending`

On worktrees: currently **bakeoff uses separate worktrees**, but **chain does not**. Chain is still serial in one checkout. That is okay for a strict linear sprint chain, but it is not good enough for the “run all unblocked plans across projects” model.

The abstraction I’d recommend:

1. **Every runnable plan gets its own worktree**
   - Branch name derived from epic/plan id: `megaplan/<epic>/<plan>`
   - Base SHA recorded at start.
   - Runtime files live outside the app repo or in ignored `.megaplan/runtime`.

2. **Dependencies are graph edges, not list order**
   - A queue finds all open plans where dependencies are satisfied.
   - Those plans can run concurrently, even across projects.
   - Epics are just containers that create or group plans; they should not be the execution primitive.

3. **Merge is a first-class state machine**
   - `running -> completed_unmerged -> merge_pending -> merged`
   - Or `merge_conflict`, `review_failed`, `discarded`.
   - Nothing is “done” until either merged or explicitly discarded.

4. **Merging should be patch/branch based**
   - For each completed worktree, capture:
     - base SHA
     - head SHA
     - full patch
     - changed files
     - tests run
     - Megaplan plan/review artifacts
   - Apply onto current integration branch with `git apply --check` or `git merge --no-ff`.
   - If conflicts happen, create a merge-resolution plan instead of silently failing.

5. **Make loss impossible by default**
   - Never delete a worktree until its patch and branch are archived.
   - Never overwrite a shared checkout with active work.
   - Never continue after failed `fetch/checkout/pull`.
   - Always expose: base ref, worktree path, branch, dirty status, merge status.

So: we fixed the immediate bug, but the architecture is not yet elegant. The right next Megaplan work is to move `chain` onto the same worktree discipline that `bakeoff` already has, then add a queue/merge layer above it. That would make chaining genuinely first-class instead of “a serial driver over one checkout.”

> DEVELOPER

and could all of that be implemented into the system elegantly? Either as mechanical or instructions for agents

> AGENT

Yes. Most of it should be **mechanical**, with agent instructions only where judgment is genuinely needed.

The elegant split:

**Mechanical System Behavior**
- Worktree creation per runnable plan.
- Branch naming, base SHA capture, dirty-state checks.
- Dependency scheduling: “run all unblocked plans.”
- Runtime state tracking: queued, running, blocked, completed, merge_pending, merged, conflicted.
- Patch capture and artifact archival.
- Merge preflight: `git apply --check`, changed-file overlap detection, test command routing.
- Refusing unsafe operations: dirty shared checkout, failed refresh, missing base, untracked runtime file collisions.
- Status output showing exactly where work lives and what needs attention.

This should not rely on agents. It is infrastructure.

**Agent-Guided Behavior**
- Deciding whether two completed plans are semantically compatible.
- Resolving merge conflicts.
- Choosing whether a failed review needs rework or can be accepted.
- Summarizing risk between concurrently produced changes.
- Creating follow-up plans when merge/review reveals a deeper architectural issue.

That means the agent should operate inside explicit lanes, not invent the process each time.

The core abstraction I’d use is probably:

```text
Epic
  owns many Plans

Plan
  has dependencies
  has target project
  has execution branch/worktree
  produces artifacts + patch

Run
  one attempt to execute a Plan
  records base SHA, head SHA, status, logs, review

Merge
  promotes a completed Run into an integration branch
  can be automatic, conflicted, or agent-assisted
```

Then the queue becomes simple:

```text
Find all Plans where:
- state = open/ready
- dependencies are merged or waived
- no conflicting active lock exists

Start each in its own worktree.
When done, review it.
If review passes, move to merge_pending.
Attempt merge.
If merge succeeds, mark merged and unblock dependents.
If merge fails, create a merge-resolution plan.
```

Agent instructions should be embedded as contracts on each phase:

- Executor: “You own only this worktree and this plan. Do not modify runtime state. Produce implementation + evidence.”
- Reviewer: “Judge against success criteria and changed files. Do not merge.”
- Merger: “Resolve only integration conflicts between these branches. Preserve both sides unless explicitly obsolete.”
- Scheduler: ideally not an agent at all.

So yes, it can be implemented cleanly. The mistake would be making “chaining” a prompt convention. It should be a real scheduler/runner/merge state machine, with agents plugged into the points where reasoning is needed.

It is making progress again now.

What happened:
- Sprint 2 had stalled on a Megaplan bug: `flags.py` crashed when an agent returned `evidence` as a list instead of a string.
- I fixed and pushed that to Megaplan: `cc504728 Handle structured flag evidence`.
- Resume then hit a second cloud friction issue: chain PR creation required `gh`, but the cloud worker doesn’t have it.
- I fixed and pushed that too: `27990e36 Skip chain PR creation when gh is unavailable`.
- Installed the latest Megaplan in the cloud worker and restarted the chain.

Current status:
- Sprint 2 critique now succeeded.
- It moved past the crash point into `gate`.
- Active step is `gate`, health `healthy`.
- The chain process and Codex process are both live.

One minor status wart: `chain_state.last_state` still says `stalled` until this Sprint 2 plan reaches a new terminal state, but the actual plan state is active and progressing.

then\n+  echo \"---plan:$PLAN---\"\n+  python -m megaplan status --plan \"$PLAN\" || true\n+  echo \"---progress:$PLAN---\"\n+  python -m megaplan progress --plan \"$PLAN\" || true\n+  echo \"---artifacts:$PLAN---\"\n+  ls -lt \".megaplan/plans/$PLAN\" 2>/dev/null | head -30 || true\n+fi\n+\n+echo \"---processes---\"\n+ps -ef | grep \"megaplan chain\\|codex exec\" | grep -v grep || true\n+\n+echo \"---git---\"\n+git status --short --branch\n+git log --oneline --decorate -5\n+\n+echo \"---branches---\"\n+git branch --show-current\n+git ls-remote origin \"refs/heads/megaplan/video-editor-dx-sprint-*\" | tail -20 || true\n+\n+echo \"---chain-log-tail---\"\n+tail -120 .megaplan/cloud-chain.log 2>/dev/null || true\n+' --cloud-yaml \"$CLOUD_YAML\"\n*** End Patch\n"

-Is) =====\"; ./.megaplan/check-cloud-status.sh; } >> .megaplan/cloud-checkins.log 2>&1; sleep 7200; done'\n+```\n+\n+Run a one-off check:\n+\n+```bash\n+cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app\n+./.megaplan/check-cloud-status.sh\n+```\n+\n+## Cloud Access\n+\n+Use the canonical local Megaplan checkout, not the old launcher clone:\n+\n+```bash\n+cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app\n+PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 PYTHONPATH=/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan \\\n+  python -m megaplan cloud exec 'cd /workspace/reigh-app; pwd; git status --short --branch' \\\n+  --cloud-yaml .megaplan/video-editor-cloud.yaml\n+```\n+\n+Attach directly to the running Railway shell through Megaplan:\n+\n+```bash\n+PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 PYTHONPATH=/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan \\\n+  python -m megaplan cloud attach --cloud-yaml .megaplan/video-editor-cloud.yaml\n+```\n+\n+Inside the cloud machine, the project is:\n+\n+```bash\n+cd /workspace/reigh-app\n+```\n+\n+The active chain tmux session is:\n+\n+```bash\n+tmux ls\n+tmux attach -t megaplan-chain\n+```\n+\n+Detach from tmux with `Ctrl-b` then `d`.\n+\n+## What To Check\n+\n+Chain progress:\n+\n+```bash\n+cd /workspace/reigh-app\n+python -m megaplan chain status --spec chain.yaml\n+cat chain_state.json\n+```\n+\n+Current plan status:\n+\n+```bash\n+PLAN=\"$(python - <<'PY'\n+import json\n+print(json.load(open(\"chain_state.json\")).get(\"current_plan_name\") or \"\")\n+PY\n+)\"\n+python -m megaplan status --plan \"$PLAN\"\n+python -m megaplan progress --plan \"$PLAN\"\n+```\n+\n+Liveness:\n+\n+```bash\n+ps -ef | grep \"megaplan chain\\|codex exec\" | grep -v grep || true\n+tmux ls\n+```\n+\n+Recent chain log:\n+\n+```bash\n+tail -200 .megaplan/cloud-chain.log\n+```\n+\n+Latest plan artifacts:\n+\n+```bash\n+ls -lt \".megaplan/plans/$PLAN\" | head -40\n+```\n+\n+Git state and pushed milestone branches:\n+\n+```bash\n+git status --short --branch\n+git log --oneline --decorate -8\n+git ls-remote origin \"refs/heads/megaplan/video-editor-dx-sprint-*\"\n+```\n+\n+## Healthy Signals\n+\n+- `megaplan chain status` shows one milestone `in_progress`.\n+- `megaplan status --plan \"$PLAN\"` has `health: healthy` when an active step is running.\n+- The active step moves through `plan`, `critique`, `gate`, `revise`, `finalize`, `execute`, and `review`.\n+- During execution, `megaplan progress --plan \"$PLAN\"` shows task and batch counters increasing.\n+- `ps` shows either `python -m megaplan chain start` alone between phases or both the chain process and a live `codex exec` process during agent work.\n+\n+## Bad Signals\n+\n+- Chain status has `last_state: stalled` and no live `codex exec` process.\n+- Plan status says `health: stale` and `lock_held: false`.\n+- `.megaplan/cloud-chain.log` shows repeated Python tracebacks or the same phase exiting non-zero.\n+- The same artifact timestamps do not change across multiple check-ins while an active step claims to be running.\n+\n+## Current Cloud Friction Found\n+\n+- The worker originally continued after git refresh failures; Megaplan now aborts refresh failures.\n+- Critique flag normalization crashed when agent evidence was structured as a list; Megaplan now coerces structured flag text.\n+- The Railway image does not have `gh`; Megaplan now skips PR creation when `gh` is unavailable and continues with branch commits/pushes.\n+- Runtime/schema files can dirty the app repo. Long term, Megaplan runtime state should live outside tracked app paths or under ignored runtime directories.\n+\n*** End Patch\n"
```json
}
```

```json
{
```
```json
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/docs/quality/megaplan_cloud_checkins.md\n@@\n-The two-hour watcher runs in local tmux session `megaplan-cloud-checkins` and writes to:\n+The two-hour watcher runs as a local background process and writes to:\n \n ```bash\n /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/cloud-checkins.log\n ```\n \n Check it locally:\n \n ```bash\n-tmux ls\n-tmux capture-pane -pt megaplan-cloud-checkins -S -200\n+cat /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/cloud-checkins.pid\n+ps -p \"$(cat /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/cloud-checkins.pid)\"\n tail -200 /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/cloud-checkins.log\n ```\n \n Restart it:\n \n ```bash\n-tmux kill-session -t megaplan-cloud-checkins 2>/dev/null || true\n-tmux new-session -d -s megaplan-cloud-checkins 'cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app && while true; do { echo; echo \"===== $(date -Is) =====\"; ./.megaplan/check-cloud-status.sh; } >> .megaplan/cloud-checkins.log 2>&1; sleep 7200; done'\n+cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app\n+if [ -f .megaplan/cloud-checkins.pid ]; then\n+  kill \"$(cat .megaplan/cloud-checkins.pid)\" 2>/dev/null || true\n+fi\n+nohup sh -c 'while true; do { echo; echo \"===== $(date -Is) =====\"; ./.megaplan/check-cloud-status.sh; } >> .megaplan/cloud-checkins.log 2>&1; sleep 7200; done' >/dev/null 2>&1 &\n+echo $! > .megaplan/cloud-checkins.pid\n ```\n*** End Patch\n"
```

```json
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/chain.py\n@@\n-Progress is persisted to ``chain_state.json`` beside the spec so a relaunched\n-process can resume where the previous run left off.\n+Progress is persisted under ``.megaplan/chains/`` so a relaunched process can\n+resume where the previous run left off without dirtying milestone branches.\n@@\n import argparse\n+import hashlib\n import json\n@@\n def _state_path_for(spec_path: Path) -> Path:\n-    return spec_path.with_name(\"chain_state.json\")\n+    spec_resolved = spec_path.resolve()\n+    digest = hashlib.sha1(str(spec_resolved).encode(\"utf-8\")).hexdigest()[:12]\n+    return spec_resolved.parent / \".megaplan\" / \"chains\" / f\"{spec_resolved.stem}-{digest}.json\"\n+\n+\n+def _legacy_state_path_for(spec_path: Path) -> Path:\n+    return spec_path.with_name(\"chain_state.json\")\n@@\n def load_chain_state(spec_path: Path) -> ChainState:\n     state_path = _state_path_for(spec_path)\n+    if not state_path.exists():\n+        legacy_path = _legacy_state_path_for(spec_path)\n+        if legacy_path.exists():\n+            state_path = legacy_path\n     if not state_path.exists():\n         return ChainState()\n@@\n def save_chain_state(spec_path: Path, state: ChainState) -> None:\n     state_path = _state_path_for(spec_path)\n+    state_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n     tmp = state_path.with_suffix(\".tmp\")\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/templates/chain.yaml.example\n@@\n-# edit as needed. What changed:
```
- Megaplan no longer writes chain runtime state beside `chain.yaml`.
- It now writes to ignored runtime storage: `.megaplan/plans/.chains/...`.
- It still reads old sibling `chain_state.json` as a legacy fallback, so existing runs can resume.
- I removed the accidentally tracked `.megaplan/chains/...` file from the Sprint 4 branch.
- I installed the fixed Megaplan commit on the Railway machine.

Commits:
- `3b3da0d3` `Keep chain state out of milestone branches`
- `afcfff3a` `Store chain state under ignored plan runtime data`

- Plan: `video-editor-developer-20260503-1435`
- Branch: `megaplan/video-editor-dx-sprint-04`
- Current step: `gate`
- Live chain/Codex processes are running.
- Worktree was clean after restart.

> AGENT

It comes from Railway service variables.

I checked:

- Local `.megaplan/video-editor-cloud.yaml` has `secrets: []`, so Megaplan is not uploading/injecting the key from that YAML.
- Railway project/service `reigh-megaplan-dev / agent` has service variables:
  - `OPENAI_API_KEY`
  - `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`
  - `GITHUB_TOKEN`
  - etc.
- Inside the worker container, `OPENAI_API_KEY` is present as an env var.

How Codex knows what to use:
- The Codex process inherits the container environment, so `OPENAI_API_KEY` is available to it.
- The worker also has `/root/.codex/auth.json` with `auth_mode: "chatgpt"` and ChatGPT tokens for `[REDACTED]`.
- So there are two auth sources present:
  1. Railway `OPENAI_API_KEY`
  2. Codex CLI ChatGPT auth file

Given `auth.json` says `auth_mode: "chatgpt"`, Codex CLI is likely using the ChatGPT login tokens for Codex sessions, while the env `OPENAI_API_KEY` is available for OpenAI SDK/API calls. The key itself is stored in Railway variables, not in the repo or Megaplan config.

If project-level profiles are available, use `all-codex`; otherwise rely on the user config where every configured phase routes to Codex.\n@@\n-- The skill docs mention `megaplan cloud`, profiles, prep/gate/finalize/revise, and newer robustness names, but the currently usable `megaplan-harness` CLI from uvx is older and only supports the legacy workflow: `clarify -> plan -> critique -> integrate -> execute -> review`.\n-- `megaplan config profiles` is unavailable in the fallback CLI.\n-- `megaplan cloud` is unavailable in the fallback CLI.\n-- The editable source checkout needed for newer functionality is blocked by filesystem privacy/TCC: listing `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan` returns `Operation not permitted`.\n-- The global `python` shim points at missing pyenv 3.8.10. Use `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11` or the repaired `megaplan` wrapper.\n+- The global `python` shim points at missing pyenv 3.8.10. Use `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11` with `python -m megaplan`.\n - Legacy fallback Codex planning has a persistent-session bug: `clarify` and `plan` share the same `codex_planner` session, but `codex exec resume` does not receive `--output-schema`, so `plan` can write markdown instead of JSON and fail parsing. Workaround: run `megaplan plan --plan <name> --agent codex --fresh` after `clarify`.\n - Legacy fallback CLI gives no streaming phase progress for long Codex phases; only final success/failure is visible.\n - The thorough legacy loop escalated without a clean way to ask for a targeted replan after max iterations. Available next steps are `override add-note`, `override force-proceed`, or `override abort`; there is no explicit `replan` command in this fallback CLI.\n+- Chain runtime state is now stored under `.megaplan/plans/.chains/` and should remain out of milestone branches.\n*** End Patch\n"

> DEVELOPER

how many more sprints to go?

> AGENT

Four more after the current one.

Current chain state:

- Done: Sprint 1, Sprint 2, Sprint 3
- In progress: Sprint 4
- Remaining after Sprint 4: Sprint 5, Sprint 6, Sprint 7, Sprint 8

So the chain has `5` sprints not fully complete yet, including Sprint 4, or `4` still to start after this one finishes.

> DEVELOPER

Wow, what are we even doing?

> AGENT

We’re running the video editor DX refactor plan through Megaplan in the Railway cloud worker.

The overall goal is to turn the current video editor from “works inside Reigh, but hard to extend” into something developers and agents can build on top of cleanly:

- Sprint 1: canonical timeline/data model
- Sprint 2: ports/adapters so Reigh-specific dependencies are named and isolated
- Sprint 3: headless/core editor separation from the Reigh shell
- Sprint 4: clip-type plugin registry, currently running
- Sprint 5: command bus and undo/redo extensibility
- Sprint 6: asset/render pipeline hooks
- Sprint 7: UI slots, theming, panel registry
- Sprint 8: public API/docs/embed demo

We also paused several times to fix Megaplan itself because cloud chaining was rough: state files were landing in the app branch, branch switching got blocked, review JSON parsing was brittle, and the worker needed the latest Megaplan code. Those fixes are now pushed too.

So, right now: the cloud worker is executing Sprint 4, which is the plugin registry piece. It has finished Sprints 1-3, and after Sprint 4 there are four more planned sprints unless we decide to stop at the MVP boundary.

> AGENT

Mostly yes. The new Reigh timeline structure is intentionally aligned with ArtAgents through the shared `@banodoco/timeline-schema` package.

What aligns:

- Top-level: `theme`, `theme_overrides`, `generation_defaults`, `clips`, `tracks`, `pinnedShotGroups`, `output`
- Clips: `id`, `at`, `track`, open-string `clipType`, `asset`, `from`, `to`, `speed`, `hold`, transforms, `params`, `generation`, `pool_id`, `clip_order`, `source_uuid`
- Assets: `assets: { [id]: entry }` with `file/url`, duration, fps, resolution, generation metadata
- ArtAgents explicitly says `artagents/timeline.py` mirrors Reigh’s `TimelineConfig`, and it preserves `generation_defaults` on round-trip.

Important caveats:

- ArtAgents uses `from_` internally because `from` is a Python keyword, but it swaps back to JSON `"from"` at load/save. That is fine, but it is a known adapter wrinkle.
- Reigh’s local type still has `output: TimelineOutput` required, while the shared schema and ArtAgents allow `output` to be optional. That is okay for persisted compatibility, but developers may see a TS mismatch.
- Reigh’s local `AssetRegistryEntry.file` is required, while the shared schema/ArtAgents allow `file` or `url`. That could annoy import/export paths.
- ArtAgents’ `remotion/node_modules/@banodoco/timeline-schema` is stale at `0.0.1`; the central shared package is `0.0.2`. The only diff I saw was `output.background` / `background_scale` nullable handling, but this should be refreshed.
- In the current ArtAgents Python env, `banodoco_timeline_schema` is not installed, so ArtAgents is falling back to its local mirrored types/validator instead of the generated shared validator. That works, but it weakens the “one source of truth” story.
- ArtAgents’ older `pool` / `arrangement` structures are separate pipeline intermediates. They map into the shared timeline, but they are not the same data model.

So: the planned/new Reigh persisted structure is aligned with the ArtAgents timeline contract, but I would not call it fully nailed until we add a small cross-repo contract test that round-trips a Reigh fixture through ArtAgents and validates it against the central `timeline-schema`.

> AGENT

The decision point is: do we accept Sprint 4 as good enough to mark complete and let Megaplan continue to Sprint 5?

My recommendation: **yes on the code direction, but clean up the branch before `verify-human`.**

Why:

- Review verdict is `approved`.
- All automatable must criteria passed.
- Focused video-editor tests passed: `70` tests for registry/manifest/render/overlay/title-card surfaces.
- Sprint 4 did what it was supposed to do: clip-type registry, descriptor-backed defaults, manifest export, title-card example, registry-backed renderer/inspector/overlay behavior.

But I would not verify it yet because the branch diff includes non-product Megaplan/runtime files:

- `chain_state.json`
- `chain.yaml`
- `.megaplan/debt.json`

That is exactly the kind of pollution we were trying to prevent. The code may be good, but the branch is not clean as a product patch.

So I’d do this:

1. Remove runtime/chain artifacts from the Sprint 4 branch.
2. Push the cleaned Sprint 4 branch.
3. Optionally do a quick browser smoke for mixed clips if we want real confidence.
4. Run `verify-human`.
5. Let the chain advance to Sprint 5.

The actual human judgment criteria are modest: does the `title-card` example really prove a developer can add a clip type through descriptor + registration, and does the editor still feel normal for text/media/effect-layer/sequence clips. Based on the review, I’d accept that after cleaning the branch.

> AGENT

Mostly yes, but not uniformly.

**Sprint 6 is probably still substantial.** Asset/render hooks touch process boundaries, preview/export behavior, worker routing, fallback behavior, and asset resolution. That’s the last “deep systems” sprint, and it can expose hidden coupling.

**Sprint 7 should be medium.** UI slots, panel registry, and theme cleanup are broad but more mechanical now that headless core, ports, registry, and command bus exist. Risk is mostly avoiding churn in `VideoEditorShell`.

**Sprint 8 should be the smallest implementation-wise, but high polish.** Public exports, docs, embed demo, fixtures, and acceptance tests. The hard part is API discipline: deciding what *not* to expose.

So in rough weight:

- Sprint 6: medium-large
- Sprint 7: medium
- Sprint 8: small-medium, mostly polish/docs/demo/testing

We’re past the hardest architectural pieces: data model, ports, headless core, plugin registry, and command bus. The remaining work should increasingly become integration and developer-experience hardening.

> AGENT

Yes. We need to restart the chain process **before Sprint 7 is initialized**.

Current state: Sprint 6 is still mid-execute, so I should **not** kill it right now. It’s at `3/11` tasks, `2/9` batches, active and healthy.

The reason: the running chain process loaded the old spec into memory. The worker package and `chain.yaml` are updated, but that existing process won’t reread them. So the clean path is:

1. Let Sprint 6 finish.
2. Stop the chain process before it starts Sprint 7.
3. Restart `megaplan chain start --spec chain.yaml`.
4. It resumes from chain state and initializes Sprint 7 with `robustness: light`.

If we miss that window and Sprint 7 starts as standard, we can still correct it, but it’s messier: stop it, set/reinit Sprint 7 as light, and avoid keeping a standard-plan artifact as the real run.

deadline = time.time() + 20\n    while time.time() < deadline:\n        if not chain_pids():\n            return\n        time.sleep(1)\n    leftovers = chain_pids()\n    if leftovers:\n        log(f\"force-killing old chain leftovers={leftovers}\")\n    for pid in leftovers:\n        try:\n            os.kill(pid, signal.SIGKILL)\n        except ProcessLookupError:\n            pass\n\n\ndef normalize_state():\n    path = chain_state_path()\n    raw = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    completed = raw.setdefault(\"completed\", [])\n    if not ROOT / \".megaplan\" / \"plans\" / stale_plan\n        if plan_dir.exists():\n            subprocess.run([\"rm\", \"-rf\", str(plan_dir)], cwd=str(ROOT), check=False)\n            log(f\"removed stale sprint7-or-later plan dir {plan_dir}\")\n    raw[\"current_milestone_index\"] = 6\n    raw[\"current_plan_name\"] = None\n    raw[\"last_state\"] = \"done\"\n    raw[\"pr_number\"] = None\n    raw[\"pr_state\"] = None\n    tmp = path.with_suffix(\".tmp\")\n    tmp.write_text(json.dumps(raw, indent=2) + \"\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    tmp.replace(path)\n    log(f\"normalized chain state at {path}\")\n\n\ndef restart_chain():\n    env = os.environ.copy()\n    env[\"MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER\"] = \"1\"\n    with open(ROOT / \".megaplan\" / \"cloud-chain.log\", \"a\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as out:\n        proc = subprocess.Popen(\n            [\"python\", \"-m\", \"megaplan\", \"chain\", \"start\", \"--spec\", \"chain.yaml\"],\n            cwd=str(ROOT),\n            env=env,\n Sprint 8 is **not cumulative** in Git ancestry. It has the final public SDK/docs/embed-demo layer, but the earlier branches still contain real work: ports/adapters, headless core, clip registry, command bus, render pipeline hooks, UI slot/runtime surfaces.

I would not blindly merge them all into this branch in one shot. They conflict with Sprint 8 in several places, and a naive merge could undo final SDK boundary work. The right path is:

1. Create/keep this as the integration branch.
2. Merge Sprint 2 through Sprint 7 sequentially.
3. Resolve conflicts with Sprint 8 as the “newer public API wins” layer.
4. Run targeted video-editor tests and SDK governance checks after each major merge.
5. Push the resulting integrated branch.
